        Idea:
# Plan 5 — Scheduled jobs + decay + pause/resume + crisis polish

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Triggers" (scheduled), "Decay", "Multi-Message Handling" (restart recovery for jobs), "Pause / Resume", "Crisis Handling", "WhatsApp 24-Hour Window" for templates, and the bits of "Operations" related to scheduled jobs.

Plans 1–4 are assumed complete. The system can take messages, run the loop with real OOB and scoring, and produce outbounds. This plan adds the time-based behaviors and the global controls (pause, crisis escalation) that the spec describes.

## Scope (in)

### Scheduled-job worker
- A single async worker (background task in the FastAPI app, or a separate Railway worker process — pick whichever is cleaner; document the choice).
- Polls `scheduled_jobs` for `status='pending' AND scheduled_for <= now()` using `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` so two workers can't fire the same job. Each job has a unique ID for idempotency.
- On fire: invoke the appropriate handler, then mark `status='fired'`, set `fired_at`. On handler error: log and leave the job pending for one retry, then mark `status='cancelled'` with a reason.

### Job types
- `weekly_summary` — per partner. Renders the Meta-approved `weekly_summary` template ("Hi {{1}}, this week we had {{2}} conversations and touched on {{3}} ongoing things. Want to talk through anything? Just ask."). Sends via `send_outbound` (which handles 24h window). Also performs the decay housekeeping pass (below). Schedule recurrences on completion (one new job per partner per week, configurable timing per user).
- `checkin` — variable check-in (4–24h, the loop chose the timing). One pending per user max. Inserting a new one supersedes any existing pending check-in for that user (mark old one `status='superseded'`). On fire: triggers the agentic loop with a `trigger_metadata={"kind": "checkin", "context": ...}` so the loop can produce a context-aware nudge or, if outside the 24h window, send the `checkin_nudge` template.
- `watch_item_due` — fires when a watch item's `due_at` arrives. Surfaces it next time the owner is in conversation, OR creates a `checkin` job at a near-future time so the bot proactively raises it. Pick one based on whether the owner is currently in the 24h window.
- `oob_review` — when an OOB's `review_at` hits. Triggers the loop with metadata so the bot asks the owner if it's still off-limits.

### Late-job handling on restart
- On startup: scan `scheduled_jobs` with `status='pending' AND scheduled_for < now()`:
  - Less than 1h late → fire normally.
  - 1–24h late → fire with `context.delayed = true` so the bot can adjust ("I meant to send this earlier...").
  - More than 24h late → mark `cancelled` with reason `"too stale"`.
- This is in addition to message-recovery from Plan 2.

### Decay housekeeping (runs inside the weekly summary job)
- Themes with no `last_active_at` update for 6+ weeks → `dormant`.
- Themes dormant 4+ months → `resolved_by_time`.
- Observations not reinforced for 3+ months → confidence drops one level (high→medium→low). Not reinforced for 6+ months → `stale`.
- Watch items past `due_at` with no addressing for 30+ days → `expired`.
- `last_reinforced_at` (not `last_active_at`) drives observation/theme decay; verify the queries reflect that.
- Also run the re-scoring pass for observations flagged `needs_rescoring=true` from Plan 4.

### Query-level decay
- Add a `recency_weighted_score(significance, last_reinforced_at)` SQL function or app-side helper. Formula per spec: `significance / (1 + age_days / 60)` where `age_days = EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - COALESCE(last_reinforced_at, created_at))) / 86400`. ~60-day effective half-life. Pure SQL, no exp() needed.
- Update the read tools' default sorts to use it. Older content remains searchable but is deprioritized in default surfacing.

### Timezone handling
- Owner-bound scheduled jobs use the owner's `users.timezone`. The recurring `weekly_summary` fires at 09:00 in the owner's local time on the configured day.
- `schedule_checkin` writes absolute UTC instants — the agentic loop converts "in 8 hours" / "tomorrow morning" to UTC at write time using the owner's timezone (timezone-aware datetime math, not naive arithmetic).
- Heartbeat and other non-user-bound jobs use UTC.

### Pause / Resume
- `/pause` slash command (literal `/pause` in inbound text — this is the only slash command per spec): on receipt from either user:
  - Cancel all pending scheduled jobs for both users (`status='superseded'`).
  - Set a global `paused_at` flag (one row in a small `system_state` table — create the table here).
  - Send pause confirmation outbound to each partner via the `pause_confirmation` template (works inside or outside 24h window).
  - The `paused_for_user(user_id)` hook from Plan 2 now reads from `system_state` and returns True when paused.
- `/resume` from either partner:
  - Clear `paused_at`.
  - Do NOT auto-process backlog — just re-enable inbound processing.
  - Restore weekly summary scheduling (insert next `weekly_summary` jobs).

### Crisis handling polish
- The system prompt already (from Plan 3) instructs the loop to drop the mediator role and consider `escalate_to_partner`. This plan tightens the escalation flow:
  - **Concrete gate per spec:** `escalate_to_partner` may only be called when (a) the triggering message meets the `crisis` charge definition, OR (b) the user explicitly asks the bot to alert their partner. The tool input requires `is_crisis: bool` and a named `reason`. Reject any call that doesn't satisfy one of these triggers — the orchestrator validates before sending. This guard is in addition to the prompt-level instruction.
  - `escalate_to_partner` writes a distinguished `bot_turns.reasoning` entry and triggers an outbound to the OTHER partner using the `escalation` template — including outside the 24h window.
  - The `{{3}}` parameter is filled by the bot with a deliberately vague contextual line per spec ("They're going through a hard moment", etc.).
  - Logs the escalation with sufficient detail for retro audit (admin view in Plan 6 will surface it).
- For acute crisis: the bot also surfaces region/language-appropriate emergency resources directly to the affected partner in the conversational outbound. Tested with a deterministic mock LLM.

### Heartbeat upgrade
- Plan 1 left a heartbeat stub. Implement it now as a periodic `scheduled_jobs` row of type `heartbeat` that fires daily and writes a log line; alerts on its absence are external (admin doc).

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Admin UI surfaces for jobs/escalations (Plan 6).
- Onboarding welcome flow (Plan 6).
- Feedback collection (Plan 6).
- Anything related to actual emergency resource curation — the spec is explicit that the LLM uses common sense for region/language; no static resource list to maintain here.

## Success criteria
- `must`: Scheduled-job worker fires `weekly_summary` for both partners on schedule, sends via `send_outbound`, and performs decay housekeeping in the same run.
- `must`: `checkin` insertion supersedes prior pending check-ins for that user.
- `must`: Late jobs on restart are categorized correctly (<1h, 1–24h, >24h) and handled per spec.
- `must`: `/pause` halts both partners symmetrically; both receive `pause_confirmation`. `/resume` re-enables.
- `must`: `escalate_to_partner` produces a templated outbound to the other partner using the `escalation` template, even outside the 24h window.
- `must`: Decay transitions (theme dormant/resolved_by_time, observation confidence drop/stale, watch items expired) execute correctly against synthetic time-traveled fixtures.
- `should`: `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` poll is verified by a concurrent-worker test.
- `should`: Recency-weighted sort is applied in default tool reads.
- `info`: `rescore_observations` is invoked from the housekeeping pass.

## Notes
- Time travel for testing: use `freezegun` or pass an injectable `now()` so decay tests don't rely on real wall clock.
- Be careful with the global pause flag — it must be checked in the agentic-loop entry path AND in scheduled-job handlers (a job firing while paused should no-op or the worker should skip pending jobs while paused; pick one approach and document).

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: debouncer callback: revised step 8 defines `burstcoalescer.add(user_id, message_id)` but says expiry calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)`. i found no existing user model or repository helper in the current codebase, and the plan does not say whether the coalescer stores the user object, fetches it from the pool, or receives it in `add`, so the callback cannot reliably supply the `user` argument required by `run_agentic_turn` as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: query-level decay: `observations.significance` is nullable in `tool_schemas.py` and in current tests for failed scoring, and `scoring.rescore_observations` can leave it null after a failed re-score. the plan's sql function `recency_weighted_score(significance, last_reinforced_at, created_at)` does not specify null handling or `nulls last`; in postgres, ordering that nullable expression `desc` can surface null-scored failed observations before valid scored observations unless the executor adds `nulls last` or `coalesce`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: escalation gate metadata: the plan says `escalate_to_partner` should accept `is_crisis=true` only when the current trigger charge is actually `crisis`, but the existing write-tool context only has `turncontext(triggering_message_ids, user, partner, phase)` and the charge is currently computed locally inside `agentic.run_agentic_turn` from hot context. the plan does not explicitly add that charge or an explicit-request marker to `turncontext` or the tool-call validator, so the proposed guard has no reliable data path in the current code shape. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] check-in-scheduling: check-in scheduling: one-pending-check-in invariant remains race-prone without a partial unique index or transactional locking strategy. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] debouncer: debouncer: coalescer cannot satisfy the `run_agentic_turn(message_ids, user)` callback contract from an `add(user_id, message_id)` api unless it stores or resolves the user object. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the search-before-write requirement against the new phase split. because phase b exposes only write/schedule tools, the model cannot recover by calling `get_memories`, `get_observations`, or `list_themes` if it realizes during phase b that it lacks enough read context to safely add versus update; this creates tension with the explicit agentic-search dedup requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: weekly summary timing: the spec requires the recurring weekly summary to fire at 09:00 local on the user's configured day, and the plan explicitly asks where that configuration should live. the implementation plan chooses global env defaults plus per-job context overrides, but `/pause` supersedes all pending jobs and `/resume` restores weekly summaries; without a durable per-user schedule field or an explicit rule to read the last superseded weekly job context, resume/startup seeding can silently lose the user's configured day. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recovery/coalescer api mismatch: revised step 14 says recovery should re-add a whole `triggering_message_ids` list as one burst, but revised step 8 only specifies `add(user_id, message_id)` for one message at a time. calling `add` repeatedly can reset timers or merge with live inbound state, so the plan needs either an explicit `add_burst(user_id, ids)`/`fire(ids)` path or clear semantics for preserving a recovered burst as a single unit. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: indexing claim: revised step 2 says the new gin index supports recovery's `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)` lookup, but postgres gin indexes on arrays are normally used by array operators such as `@>`, `&&`, or `<@`, not by scalar `= any(array)` in a correlated subquery. the executor should either use an indexable predicate like `triggering_message_ids @> array[messages.id]` or drop the claim that this query is gin-backed. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked audit support around `prompt_snapshot`. the revised step 8 still opens `bot_turns` with `prompt_snapshot` before building hot context, while the spec's worked example calls for `<full prompt>` after hot context is loaded; unless the executor reorders this, the stored prompt snapshot will omit the per-turn hot context actually injected into the llm. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: check-in uniqueness: the success criteria require one pending check-in per user, and the current `schedule_checkin` implementation performs a separate `update scheduled_jobs set status='superseded'` followed by `insert into scheduled_jobs`. the plan later says to add db-level coverage, but the migration step only mentions indexes and does not specify a partial unique index such as one pending check-in per user, so two concurrent schedule calls can still race and leave multiple pending check-ins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] edit-handling: edit handling: edit events that reuse the original whatsapp message id can be swallowed by the normal idempotent insert path unless edit detection is explicitly performed before duplicate-message handling. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate: escalation gate: the plan does not define how the write tool receives authoritative trigger charge or explicit-request evidence. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate-ordering: escalation gate ordering: the revised `escalate_to_partner` step sends the partner outbound before the plan text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the new `hooks.check_oob` default against existing tests. `tests/test_send_outbound.py` has an autouse fixture that sets the hook to `none`, so preserving an intentional no-hook test path is compatible with the plan's module-level default, but the new plan 3 e2e test must install a counting hook if it wants to assert the pass-through was invoked. - [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the plan proposes replacing `hooks.paused_for_user()` with a db-backed implementation and notes that changing call sites to pass `pool` is cleaner. grep shows current direct callers in `messaging.send_outbound`, `inbound.process_inbound`, and `agentic.run_agentic_turn`; tests also monkeypatch a one-argument hook. if the executor chooses the cleaner signature change, all these callers and tests must be updated together, and media handlers still need separate pause coverage as noted in the scope finding. - [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `send_outbound` callers are `agentic.run_agentic_turn`, `write_tools.escalate_to_partner`, and tests, and the function currently accepts only `template_fallback` and `bot_turn_id` keyword arguments. adding an `ignore_pause` keyword is compatible if defaulted to false, but `/pause` confirmation send sites must pass it explicitly or the confirmation rows will be withheld immediately after the global pause flag is set. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] media-state: media state: the plan should explicitly keep successfully media-enriched inbound messages in `processing_state='raw'` until the agentic loop consumes them. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] pause-resume: pause/resume: media inbound paths can bypass the pause gate and enqueue voice/image messages after enrichment. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] query-level-decay: query-level decay: nullable observation significance can break default weighted ordering unless null scores are explicitly handled. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

If a test fails and its ID appears in baseline_test_failures, it is pre-existing — do not scope-creep into fixing it. If baseline_test_failures is null, the baseline could not be captured; use your judgment but err on the side of assuming failures are regressions. You MUST still re-run the FULL test suite with your changes applied — pre-existing failures do not excuse skipping verification. Never narrow to individual test functions and stop.
        - If this batch includes the final verification task, write a short script that reproduces the exact bug described in the task, run it to confirm the fix resolves it, then delete the script.

        Idea:
# Plan 5 — Scheduled jobs + decay + pause/resume + crisis polish

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Triggers" (scheduled), "Decay", "Multi-Message Handling" (restart recovery for jobs), "Pause / Resume", "Crisis Handling", "WhatsApp 24-Hour Window" for templates, and the bits of "Operations" related to scheduled jobs.

Plans 1–4 are assumed complete. The system can take messages, run the loop with real OOB and scoring, and produce outbounds. This plan adds the time-based behaviors and the global controls (pause, crisis escalation) that the spec describes.

## Scope (in)

### Scheduled-job worker
- A single async worker (background task in the FastAPI app, or a separate Railway worker process — pick whichever is cleaner; document the choice).
- Polls `scheduled_jobs` for `status='pending' AND scheduled_for <= now()` using `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` so two workers can't fire the same job. Each job has a unique ID for idempotency.
- On fire: invoke the appropriate handler, then mark `status='fired'`, set `fired_at`. On handler error: log and leave the job pending for one retry, then mark `status='cancelled'` with a reason.

### Job types
- `weekly_summary` — per partner. Renders the Meta-approved `weekly_summary` template ("Hi {{1}}, this week we had {{2}} conversations and touched on {{3}} ongoing things. Want to talk through anything? Just ask."). Sends via `send_outbound` (which handles 24h window). Also performs the decay housekeeping pass (below). Schedule recurrences on completion (one new job per partner per week, configurable timing per user).
- `checkin` — variable check-in (4–24h, the loop chose the timing). One pending per user max. Inserting a new one supersedes any existing pending check-in for that user (mark old one `status='superseded'`). On fire: triggers the agentic loop with a `trigger_metadata={"kind": "checkin", "context": ...}` so the loop can produce a context-aware nudge or, if outside the 24h window, send the `checkin_nudge` template.
- `watch_item_due` — fires when a watch item's `due_at` arrives. Surfaces it next time the owner is in conversation, OR creates a `checkin` job at a near-future time so the bot proactively raises it. Pick one based on whether the owner is currently in the 24h window.
- `oob_review` — when an OOB's `review_at` hits. Triggers the loop with metadata so the bot asks the owner if it's still off-limits.

### Late-job handling on restart
- On startup: scan `scheduled_jobs` with `status='pending' AND scheduled_for < now()`:
  - Less than 1h late → fire normally.
  - 1–24h late → fire with `context.delayed = true` so the bot can adjust ("I meant to send this earlier...").
  - More than 24h late → mark `cancelled` with reason `"too stale"`.
- This is in addition to message-recovery from Plan 2.

### Decay housekeeping (runs inside the weekly summary job)
- Themes with no `last_active_at` update for 6+ weeks → `dormant`.
- Themes dormant 4+ months → `resolved_by_time`.
- Observations not reinforced for 3+ months → confidence drops one level (high→medium→low). Not reinforced for 6+ months → `stale`.
- Watch items past `due_at` with no addressing for 30+ days → `expired`.
- `last_reinforced_at` (not `last_active_at`) drives observation/theme decay; verify the queries reflect that.
- Also run the re-scoring pass for observations flagged `needs_rescoring=true` from Plan 4.

### Query-level decay
- Add a `recency_weighted_score(significance, last_reinforced_at)` SQL function or app-side helper. Formula per spec: `significance / (1 + age_days / 60)` where `age_days = EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - COALESCE(last_reinforced_at, created_at))) / 86400`. ~60-day effective half-life. Pure SQL, no exp() needed.
- Update the read tools' default sorts to use it. Older content remains searchable but is deprioritized in default surfacing.

### Timezone handling
- Owner-bound scheduled jobs use the owner's `users.timezone`. The recurring `weekly_summary` fires at 09:00 in the owner's local time on the configured day.
- `schedule_checkin` writes absolute UTC instants — the agentic loop converts "in 8 hours" / "tomorrow morning" to UTC at write time using the owner's timezone (timezone-aware datetime math, not naive arithmetic).
- Heartbeat and other non-user-bound jobs use UTC.

### Pause / Resume
- `/pause` slash command (literal `/pause` in inbound text — this is the only slash command per spec): on receipt from either user:
  - Cancel all pending scheduled jobs for both users (`status='superseded'`).
  - Set a global `paused_at` flag (one row in a small `system_state` table — create the table here).
  - Send pause confirmation outbound to each partner via the `pause_confirmation` template (works inside or outside 24h window).
  - The `paused_for_user(user_id)` hook from Plan 2 now reads from `system_state` and returns True when paused.
- `/resume` from either partner:
  - Clear `paused_at`.
  - Do NOT auto-process backlog — just re-enable inbound processing.
  - Restore weekly summary scheduling (insert next `weekly_summary` jobs).

### Crisis handling polish
- The system prompt already (from Plan 3) instructs the loop to drop the mediator role and consider `escalate_to_partner`. This plan tightens the escalation flow:
  - **Concrete gate per spec:** `escalate_to_partner` may only be called when (a) the triggering message meets the `crisis` charge definition, OR (b) the user explicitly asks the bot to alert their partner. The tool input requires `is_crisis: bool` and a named `reason`. Reject any call that doesn't satisfy one of these triggers — the orchestrator validates before sending. This guard is in addition to the prompt-level instruction.
  - `escalate_to_partner` writes a distinguished `bot_turns.reasoning` entry and triggers an outbound to the OTHER partner using the `escalation` template — including outside the 24h window.
  - The `{{3}}` parameter is filled by the bot with a deliberately vague contextual line per spec ("They're going through a hard moment", etc.).
  - Logs the escalation with sufficient detail for retro audit (admin view in Plan 6 will surface it).
- For acute crisis: the bot also surfaces region/language-appropriate emergency resources directly to the affected partner in the conversational outbound. Tested with a deterministic mock LLM.

### Heartbeat upgrade
- Plan 1 left a heartbeat stub. Implement it now as a periodic `scheduled_jobs` row of type `heartbeat` that fires daily and writes a log line; alerts on its absence are external (admin doc).

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Admin UI surfaces for jobs/escalations (Plan 6).
- Onboarding welcome flow (Plan 6).
- Feedback collection (Plan 6).
- Anything related to actual emergency resource curation — the spec is explicit that the LLM uses common sense for region/language; no static resource list to maintain here.

## Success criteria
- `must`: Scheduled-job worker fires `weekly_summary` for both partners on schedule, sends via `send_outbound`, and performs decay housekeeping in the same run.
- `must`: `checkin` insertion supersedes prior pending check-ins for that user.
- `must`: Late jobs on restart are categorized correctly (<1h, 1–24h, >24h) and handled per spec.
- `must`: `/pause` halts both partners symmetrically; both receive `pause_confirmation`. `/resume` re-enables.
- `must`: `escalate_to_partner` produces a templated outbound to the other partner using the `escalation` template, even outside the 24h window.
- `must`: Decay transitions (theme dormant/resolved_by_time, observation confidence drop/stale, watch items expired) execute correctly against synthetic time-traveled fixtures.
- `should`: `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` poll is verified by a concurrent-worker test.
- `should`: Recency-weighted sort is applied in default tool reads.
- `info`: `rescore_observations` is invoked from the housekeeping pass.

## Notes
- Time travel for testing: use `freezegun` or pass an injectable `now()` so decay tests don't rely on real wall clock.
- Be careful with the global pause flag — it must be checked in the agentic-loop entry path AND in scheduled-job handlers (a job firing while paused should no-op or the worker should skip pending jobs while paused; pick one approach and document).

  "watch_items": [
    "Durable weekly-summary timing is mandatory: pause/resume must not lose a user's configured day/time. Prefer explicit per-user fields or a clearly durable source over pending-job context alone.",

    "Pause must cover all enqueue paths, including voice and image handlers after media enrichment, not just text/document inbound branches.",
    "The one-pending-check-in invariant is race-prone without a partial unique index or equivalent transactional locking. Add DB enforcement and handle conflicts intentionally.",
    "Escalation validation needs authoritative context. Add trigger charge and explicit-alert-request evidence to `TurnContext` or another trusted validator input before the write tool runs.",
    "Escalation gate must run before any outbound send. This is an existing debt item from prior plans.",
    "Pause confirmations must bypass pause withholding by passing `ignore_pause=True`; ordinary outbound should still respect pause.",
    "If `paused_for_user` signature changes, update all callers and tests together: messaging, inbound, agentic, media paths, and monkeypatch fixtures.",
    "Scheduled worker should leave user-facing jobs pending while paused or otherwise avoid firing user messages; heartbeat should still run.",
    "Late recovery should only annotate/cancel jobs and let the worker perform normal dispatch to preserve retry/idempotency semantics.",
    "Do not mark successfully enriched media messages processed before the agentic loop consumes them; preserve the raw-message contract from prior plans.",

      "question": "Does the migration/config layer include every durable field and constraint needed for scheduler retries, heartbeat, pause state, rescoring, per-user weekly timing, null-safe recency scoring, and one pending check-in per user?",
      "executor_note": "Yes. The weighted score deliberately is not directly indexed because it depends on now(); a supporting active-observation index was added instead.",
      "verdict": ""
    },
    {
      "id": "SC2",
      "task_id": "T2",
      "question": "Can two workers claim due jobs without double-firing, and does startup recovery classify late jobs exactly as under 1h, 1-24h delayed, and over 24h cancelled with `too stale`?",
      "executor_note": "Yes. Due-job claiming is a single UPDATE fed by SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED and marks claimed_at/claimed_by before dispatch, preventing double claims across workers. Startup recovery leaves <1h late jobs pending, marks 1-24h late jobs delayed in context and delayed column, and cancels >24h late jobs with cancellation_reason='too stale'.",
      "verdict": ""
    },

      "executor_note": "Yes. weekly_summary, checkin, watch_item_due, oob_review, and heartbeat all have registered handlers.       "verdict": ""
    },

    "iteration": 1,
    "idea": "# Plan 5 \u2014 Scheduled jobs + decay + pause/resume + crisis polish\n\nAuthoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: \"Triggers\" (scheduled), \"Decay\", \"Multi-Message Handling\" (restart recovery for jobs), \"Pause / Resume\", \"Crisis Handling\", \"WhatsApp 24-Hour Window\" for templates, and the bits of \"Operations\" related to scheduled jobs.\n\nPlans 1\u20134 are assumed complete. The system can take messages, run the loop with real OOB and scoring, and produce outbounds. This plan adds the time-based behaviors and the global controls (pause, crisis escalation) that the spec describes.\n\n## Scope (in)\n\n### Scheduled-job worker\n- A single async worker (background task in the FastAPI app, or a separate Railway worker process \u2014 pick whichever is cleaner; document the choice).\n- Polls `scheduled_jobs` for `status='pending' AND scheduled_for <= now()` using `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` so two workers can't fire the same job. Each job has a unique ID for idempotency.\n- On fire: invoke the appropriate handler, then mark `status='fired'`, set `fired_at`. On handler error: log and leave the job pending for one retry, then mark `status='cancelled'` with a reason.\n\n### Job types\n- `weekly_summary` \u2014 per partner. Renders the Meta-approved `weekly_summary` template (\"Hi {{1}}, this week we had {{2}} conversations and touched on {{3}} ongoing things. Want to talk through anything? Just ask.\"). Sends via `send_outbound` (which handles 24h window). Also performs the decay housekeeping pass (below). Schedule recurrences on completion (one new job per partner per week, configurable timing per user).\n- `checkin` \u2014 variable check-in (4\u201324h, the loop chose the timing). One pending per user max. Inserting a new one supersedes any existing pending check-in for that user (mark old one `status='superseded'`). On fire: triggers the agentic loop with a `trigger_metadata={\"kind\": \"checkin\", \"context\": ...}` so the loop can produce a context-aware nudge or, if outside the 24h window, send the `checkin_nudge` template.\n- `watch_item_due` \u2014 fires when a watch item's `due_at` arrives. Surfaces it next time the owner is in conversation, OR creates a `checkin` job at a near-future time so the bot proactively raises it. Pick one based on whether the owner is currently in the 24h window.\n- `oob_review` \u2014 when an OOB's `review_at` hits. Triggers the loop with metadata so the bot asks the owner if it's still off-limits.\n\n### Late-job handling on restart\n- On startup: scan `scheduled_jobs` with `status='pending' AND scheduled_for < now()`:\n  - Less than 1h late \u2192 fire normally.\n  - 1\u201324h late \u2192 fire with `context.delayed = true` so the bot can adjust (\"I meant to send this earlier...\").\n  - More than 24h late \u2192 mark `cancelled` with reason `\"too stale\"`.\n- This is in addition to message-recovery from Plan 2.\n\n### Decay housekeeping (runs inside the weekly summary job)\n- Themes with no `last_active_at` update for 6+ weeks \u2192 `dormant`.\n- Themes dormant 4+ months \u2192 `resolved_by_time`.\n- Observations not reinforced for 3+ months \u2192 confidence drops one level (high\u2192medium\u2192low). Not reinforced for 6+ months \u2192 `stale`.\n- Watch items past `due_at` with no addressing for 30+ days \u2192 `expired`.\n- `last_reinforced_at` (not `last_active_at`) drives observation/theme decay; verify the queries reflect that.\n- Also run the re-scoring pass for observations flagged `needs_rescoring=true` from Plan 4.\n\n### Query-level decay\n- Add a `recency_weighted_score(significance, last_reinforced_at)` SQL function or app-side helper. Formula per spec: `significance / (1 + age_days / 60)` where `age_days = EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - COALESCE(last_reinforced_at, created_at))) / 86400`. ~60-day effective half-life. Pure SQL, no exp() needed.\n- Update the read tools' default sorts to use it. Older content remains searchable but is deprioritized in default surfacing.\n\n### Timezone handling\n- Owner-bound scheduled jobs use the owner's `users.timezone`. The recurring `weekly_summary` fires at 09:00 in the owner's local time on the configured day.\n- `schedule_checkin` writes absolute UTC instants \u2014 the agentic loop converts \"in 8 hours\" / \"tomorrow morning\" to UTC at write time using the owner's timezone (timezone-aware datetime math, not naive arithmetic).\n- Heartbeat and other non-user-bound jobs use UTC.\n\n### Pause / Resume\n- `/pause` slash command (literal `/pause` in inbound text \u2014 this is the only slash command per spec): on receipt from either user:\n  - Cancel all pending scheduled jobs for both users (`status='superseded'`).\n  - Set a global `paused_at` flag (one row in a small `system_state` table \u2014 create the table here).\n  - Send pause confirmation outbound to each partner via the `pause_confirmation` template (works inside or outside 24h window).\n  - The `paused_for_user(user_id)` hook from Plan 2 now reads from `system_state` and returns True when paused.\n- `/resume` from either partner:\n  - Clear `paused_at`.\n  - Do NOT auto-process backlog \u2014 just re-enable inbound processing.\n  - Restore weekly summary scheduling (insert next `weekly_summary` jobs).\n\n### Crisis handling polish\n- The system prompt already (from Plan 3) instructs the loop to drop the mediator role and consider `escalate_to_partner`. This plan tightens the escalation flow:\n  - **Concrete gate per spec:** `escalate_to_partner` may only be called when (a) the triggering message meets the `crisis` charge definition, OR (b) the user explicitly asks the bot to alert their partner. The tool input requires `is_crisis: bool` and a named `reason`. Reject any call that doesn't satisfy one of these triggers \u2014 the orchestrator validates before sending. This guard is in addition to the prompt-level instruction.\n  - `escalate_to_partner` writes a distinguished `bot_turns.reasoning` entry and triggers an outbound to the OTHER partner using the `escalation` template \u2014 including outside the 24h window.\n  - The `{{3}}` parameter is filled by the bot with a deliberately vague contextual line per spec (\"They're going through a hard moment\", etc.).\n  - Logs the escalation with sufficient detail for retro audit (admin view in Plan 6 will surface it).\n- For acute crisis: the bot also surfaces region/language-appropriate emergency resources directly to the affected partner in the conversational outbound. Tested with a deterministic mock LLM.\n\n### Heartbeat upgrade\n- Plan 1 left a heartbeat stub. Implement it now as a periodic `scheduled_jobs` row of type `heartbeat` that fires daily and writes a log line; alerts on its absence are external (admin doc).\n\n## Scope (out, deliberately)\n- Admin UI surfaces for jobs/escalations (Plan 6).\n- Onboarding welcome flow (Plan 6).\n- Feedback collection (Plan 6).\n- Anything related to actual emergency resource curation \u2014 the spec is explicit that the LLM uses common sense for region/language; no static resource list to maintain here.\n\n## Success criteria\n- `must`: Scheduled-job worker fires `weekly_summary` for both partners on schedule, sends via `send_outbound`, and performs decay housekeeping in the same run.\n- `must`: `checkin` insertion supersedes prior pending check-ins for that user.\n- `must`: Late jobs on restart are categorized correctly (<1h, 1\u201324h, >24h) and handled per spec.\n- `must`: `/pause` halts both partners symmetrically; both receive `pause_confirmation`. `/resume` re-enables.\n- `must`: `escalate_to_partner` produces a templated outbound to the other partner using the `escalation` template, even outside the 24h window.\n- `must`: Decay transitions (theme dormant/resolved_by_time, observation confidence drop/stale, watch items expired) execute correctly against synthetic time-traveled fixtures.\n- `should`: `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` poll is verified by a concurrent-worker test.\n- `should`: Recency-weighted sort is applied in default tool reads.\n- `info`: `rescore_observations` is invoked from the housekeeping pass.\n\n## Notes\n- Time travel for testing: use `freezegun` or pass an injectable `now()` so decay tests don't rely on real wall clock.\n- Be careful with the global pause flag \u2014 it must be checked in the agentic-loop entry path AND in scheduled-job handlers (a job firing while paused should no-op or the worker should skip pending jobs while paused; pick one approach and document).",

        Requirements:
- Implement the intent, not just the text.
- Adapt if repository reality contradicts the plan.
- Report deviations explicitly.
- Do not over-engineer beyond what the plan prescribes — no str() wraps, .get() fallbacks, or try/except guards unless the plan called for them or you found a concrete reason.
- Do NOT fix unrelated issues you encounter (e.g., dependency compatibility, Python version workarounds). Only change files directly needed for the task. If tests need updating, only update tests that are directly related to your fix.
- If you cannot build the project from source (e.g., C extension compilation failures), report the build failure explicitly. Do NOT fall back to testing against an installed or cached package — that tests the wrong codebase and produces false positives.
- If you cannot verify your changes (tests missing or unrunnable), treat this as high risk — re-examine your implementation with extra scrutiny instead of accepting it on faith.
- If tests fail, read the traceback carefully. Diagnose WHY — don't just retry. Common causes: wrong function/method used, missing import, incorrect type, edge case not handled. Fix the root cause, then re-run.
- When verifying changes, run the entire test file or module (e.g., `pytest tests/test_foo.py`), not individual test functions. Individual tests miss regressions in the same module.
- finalize.json includes baseline_test_failures — a list of test IDs that were already failing before your changes.         Sense checks to keep in mind during execution (reviewer will verify these):
- SC1 (T1): Does the migration/config layer include every durable field and constraint needed for scheduler retries, heartbeat, pause state, rescoring, per-user weekly timing, null-safe recency scoring, and one pending check-in per user?
- SC2 (T2): Can two workers claim due jobs without double-firing, and does startup recovery classify late jobs exactly as under 1h, 1-24h delayed, and over 24h cancelled with `too stale`?
- SC3 (T3): Does each job type dispatch to the specified behavior, and do weekly summaries both send the approved template and schedule the next local-09:00 recurrence from durable user timing?
- SC4 (T4): Do decay queries use `last_reinforced_at` or `created_at` where required, avoid `last_active_at` for observation decay, and clear `needs_rescoring` only after successful rescoring?
- SC5 (T5): While paused, are inbound messages of every supported type persisted but prevented from entering the coalescer/agentic loop, and do both partners receive pause confirmation despite pause being active?
- SC6 (T6): Do default observation reads and hot context sort by recency-weighted score without allowing null significance rows to sort above valid scored rows?
- SC7 (T7): Can `escalate_to_partner` only send after a trusted crisis trigger or explicit partner-alert request is proven, and is the guard executed before any template outbound?
- SC8 (T8): Do the focused tests exercise all Plan 5 success criteria plus the critique flags: weekly timing persistence, null weighted sort, media pause paths, check-in uniqueness, and escalation metadata?
- SC9 (T9): Is the in-process scheduler choice and external heartbeat alerting expectation documented clearly enough for a future deploy/operator to understand the runtime model?
- SC10 (T10): Do targeted tests, full `pytest`, and the deleted throwaway reproduction script all pass after fixes, with no lingering generated test artifacts?
- SC11 (T11): Were all after_execute user_actions clearly surfaced to the user without the executor performing them?
Watch items to keep visible during execution:
- Durable weekly-summary timing is mandatory: pause/resume must not lose a user's configured day/time. Prefer explicit per-user fields or a clearly durable source over pending-job context alone.
- Observation significance is nullable. Weighted ordering must use `NULLS LAST` or `COALESCE`, and tests should include failed/unscored observations.
- Pause must cover all enqueue paths, including voice and image handlers after media enrichment, not just text/document inbound branches.
- The one-pending-check-in invariant is race-prone without a partial unique index or equivalent transactional locking. Add DB enforcement and handle conflicts intentionally.
- Escalation validation needs authoritative context. Add trigger charge and explicit-alert-request evidence to `TurnContext` or another trusted validator input before the write tool runs.
- Escalation gate must run before any outbound send. This is an existing debt item from prior plans.
- Pause confirmations must bypass pause withholding by passing `ignore_pause=True`; ordinary outbound should still respect pause.
- If `paused_for_user` signature changes, update all callers and tests together: messaging, inbound, agentic, media paths, and monkeypatch fixtures.
- Scheduled worker should leave user-facing jobs pending while paused or otherwise avoid firing user messages; heartbeat should still run.
- Late recovery should only annotate/cancel jobs and let the worker perform normal dispatch to preserve retry/idempotency semantics.
- Do not mark successfully enriched media messages processed before the agentic loop consumes them; preserve the raw-message contract from prior plans.
- Keep scheduler and decay logic in testable service modules, with FastAPI startup limited to lifecycle wiring.
- No static emergency-resource database is in scope; crisis-resource language remains LLM/mock driven.
- The final validation task should not add more test files. Add planned tests in T8, then use the final task only to run tests and a deleted throwaway reproduction script.

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: query-level decay: `observations.significance` is nullable in `tool_schemas.py` and in current tests for failed scoring, and `scoring.rescore_observations` can leave it null after a failed re-score. the plan's sql function `recency_weighted_score(significance, last_reinforced_at, created_at)` does not specify null handling or `nulls last`; in postgres, ordering that nullable expression `desc` can surface null-scored failed observations before valid scored observations unless the executor adds `nulls last` or `coalesce`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: escalation gate metadata: the plan says `escalate_to_partner` should accept `is_crisis=true` only when the current trigger charge is actually `crisis`, but the existing write-tool context only has `turncontext(triggering_message_ids, user, partner, phase)` and the charge is currently computed locally inside `agentic.run_agentic_turn` from hot context. the plan does not explicitly add that charge or an explicit-request marker to `turncontext` or the tool-call validator, so the proposed guard has no reliable data path in the current code shape. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
